# AI Media Scout — 2026-10-21

Today’s edition adds 50 distinct tools to the library and completes 18 earlier screened entries as detailed guides. It covers AI-assisted media editing, voice production, score transcription, object relighting, embroidery, CAD, terrain and interactive scenes. Every selected entry has a full software-license review and a scoped quality assessment. Model weights, assets, paid services and proprietary hosts retain separate terms; documentation review does not establish a recommendation. All platforms receive equal consideration, with support reported only where documented. No discovered app was installed or run. The complete retained catalog received metadata-level scanning, followed by bounded manual metadata review, 146 targeted repository source snapshots, a GitLab-hosted terrain review and a final pass beyond the automatic README shortlist. This is not a manual eligibility review of every raw result. Unreviewed or excluded candidates stay outside the eligible library. All 489 planned repository queries and 18 model-task queries were attempted across 63 fields. Repository outcomes: 489 successful, 0 partial and 0 failed; 111 reached result bounds. Model-task failures: 0. The collection records 127 warnings, including bounded searches and source retrieval limitations. All eight configured web ecosystems have findings or explicit gaps; Codeberg and SourceHut remain access gaps. Coverage is not exhaustive. A focused existing-library review confirms restrictive inherited FastGS source terms; its license evidence is marked failed without changing sealed historical reports. The weekly whole-library pass starts October 26 and is not due today.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Field | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Images & design | Reviewed local generation, guided image editing and versioned research generators. OpenDesign is held for altered license wording; an SPDX badge alone did not clear it. [Source 1](https://github.com/PurpleDoubleD/locally-uncensored) [Source 2](https://github.com/ai-forever/Kandinsky-2) [Source 3](https://github.com/nexu-io/open-design/blob/main/LICENSE) |
| Video, animation & film | Reviewed captioning, clip selection, storyboards and model orchestration. Local media processing often still depends on remote ranking or generation services. [Source 1](https://github.com/yudgunH/XinChao-Cut) [Source 2](https://github.com/storybored-app/storybored) [Source 3](https://github.com/Anil-matcha/AI-Youtube-Shorts-Generator) |
| Audio, music & voice | Completed voice-conversion, dubbing and audiobook guides. Maintainer examples and model availability do not establish reliable pronunciation or permissive voice-weight terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/finrandojin/alexandria-audiobook) [Source 2](https://github.com/ThioJoe/Auto-Synced-Translated-Dubs) |
| 3D, reconstruction & assets | Reviewed single-image/video dataset preparation, reconstruction and splat viewing. Image2Splat requires a separate trainer; FastGS retains restrictive inherited code terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/romanpillai/Image2Splat) [Source 2](https://github.com/fastgs/FastGS/blob/main/LICENSE_ORIGINAL.md) |
| Browser tools & web media | Reviewed AI website builders and local creative workbenches. A browser interface does not establish support for every native OS, and a local editor may require a separately deployed AI endpoint. [Source 1](https://github.com/buildingopen/openpage) [Source 2](https://voxelsite.com/) |
| WebXR, VR & AR | Stereoscopic and spatial reconstruction sources remain relevant to immersive viewing. Headset output compatibility does not by itself clear the source or model license; no newly validated end-to-end headset route is claimed. [Source 1](https://github.com/TencentARC/StereoCrafter) [Source 2](https://github.com/garethnisbet/LiDARStudio) |
| Computational art & creative coding | Reviewed learned media embeddings, image synthesis and agent-driven asset pipelines. Conventional graphics alone were not accepted as AI functionality. [Source 1](https://openl3.readthedocs.io/en/latest/tutorial.html) [Source 2](https://github.com/FoundationVision/LlamaGen) |
| Interactive, immersive & live media | Tangible documents narrated interactive scenes, while InternGPT documents pointing-driven image edits. Each has distinct backend, setup and maturity limits. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT) |
| 3D printing & generative CAD | AI embroidery and agent-authored CAD were promising. Machine-file export and watertight-mesh claims are not evidence that a physical output has been validated. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry) [Source 2](https://github.com/bhaktofmahakal/text-to-cad) |
| Games & production pipelines | Reviewed agent-authored pixel art and video-to-sprite conversion, with explicit engine/export boundaries. A generic procedural game was not treated as an AI tool without a concrete model or agent workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool) [Source 2](https://sprites.trebeljahr.com/) |
| Motion capture & character animation | ARDY provides text and kinematic control for human motion. Its tested NVIDIA/Linux setup and model access requirements are separate from the Apache software grant. [Source 1](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/) |
| Avatars, digital humans & lip sync | Reviewed voiced web characters and portrait services. Placeholder imagery, optional external lip-sync engines and separately governed face/voice models remain explicit limitations. | VFX, compositing & relighting | SeedVR2 and the archive-remaster orchestrator offer restoration workflows. Their platform claims are kept distinct. [Source 1](https://github.com/FernandoAbishai/ScriptCut/blob/main/docs/PLATFORM_SUPPORT.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/yudgunH/XinChao-Cut) |
| Vector graphics, illustration & textures | Embroidery paths, sprite metadata and archaeological drawing/vectorization were researched. | AI color direction and palette-based media | Reference-guided archive colorization and recolorable scene research were reviewed. Other converter licenses remain unresolved. [Source 1](https://github.com/TencentARC/StereoCrafter) [Source 2](https://github.com/jdmarinv/3D-Converter) |
| AI semantic editing of captured 3D scenes | Added this field and targeted queries before collection. Reviewed splat authoring/reconstruction tools and ArtisanGS/ManifoldSplat research; a paper without cleared released code remains a lead for future investigation. [Source 1](https://github.com/garethnisbet/LiDARStudio) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10173) [Source 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03599) |

## Search and review counts

63 creative fields; 489/489 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 25819 distinct source candidates and 850 model leads. 50 detailed profiles and 18 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 111 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 2 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Read LibreTerrain’s official README and both complete root software licenses through the public GitLab API; its assistant-controlled terrain workflow qualifies. MotionNet remains a license gap after its attempted main-branch LICENSE path returned 404. [Source](https://gitlab.com/Roxanne_Ardary/motionnet) [Source](https://gitlab.com/Roxanne_Ardary/motionnet/-/raw/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://gitlab.com/libreterrain/libreterrain/-/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/libreterrain/libreterrain/-/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)
- **codeberg** · gap: Live searches and an attempted Imaginer project read were blocked by robots restrictions in the web tool. No current Codeberg source review or unchanged-project refresh is claimed. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer)
- **sourcehut** · gap: Live searches and a direct diffuser project read did not yield accessible primary source content through the web tool. This is a source-access gap, not evidence that no tools exist. [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser)
- **packages** · searched: Read OpenL3’s PyPI distribution documentation and tutorial; also followed the sprite-tools npm-linked package workflow. Package metadata and model terms remain distinct from a full software-license review. [Source](https://pypi.org/project/openl3/) [Source](https://sprites.trebeljahr.com/docs/install)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Reviewed the SeedVR2 ComfyUI integration, ComfyUI-backed storyboard workflows and sprite export/MCP tools. Host, model, runtime and asset terms are recorded separately. [Source](https://github.com/numz/ComfyUI-SeedVR2_VideoUpscaler) [Source](https://github.com/storybored-app/storybored) [Source](https://sprites.trebeljahr.com/)
- **project-sites** · searched: Supplemented all 63 fields with live primary-source web searches, including terrain, motion, acoustic, perceptual-art and olfactory project pages. Search snippets, inaccessible pages and papers without released code were not treated as completed tool reviews. [Source](https://libreterrain.org/) [Source](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/) [Source](https://diffusionillusions.com/) [Source](https://www.media.mit.edu/projects/anemoia-device/overview/)
- **research-code** · searched: Followed motion, splat, painting, music notation, holography and learned-codec sources. A second repository pass followed newly surfaced primary web links. Restricted or incomplete licenses remain holds; research provenance does not assign Experimental. [Source](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03599) [Source](https://github.com/tensorflow/moonlight) [Source](https://github.com/computational-imaging/neural-holography)
- **international** · searched: Read Korean comic-workshop text, Chinese digital-human and kinetic-installation documentation, Vietnamese-authored video-editor documentation and multilingual model/application instructions. Language, geography and popularity were not quality criteria. [Source](https://github.com/bloglluukk-glitch/comic-studio-starter) [Source](https://github.com/lanzhu1993/Digital-Human) [Source](https://github.com/dattodigm/DATT3700) [Source](https://github.com/yudgunH/XinChao-Cut)

## StitchFoundry

3D printing & generative CAD · AI-assisted textile and computational craft · Vector graphics, illustration & textures · Images & design

First guide in this library; the repository was created within the discovery window. Repository age does not establish a new product launch or quality.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

The license and creative AI workflow are supported; testing and independent-use gaps leave quality unverified. Pre-release status or ongoing development alone is not treated as proof of unstable output.

Scope: Developing a motif, choosing thread colors and planning stitch sequences for machine embroidery.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-21. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README shows the interface, example designs and a browser demo. These are author examples; no sewn production sample was verified in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the official macOS universal DMG, Windows x64 MSI/portable package or Linux AppImage/deb/rpm. For source development, use the documented Rust toolchain. Configure your own Anthropic key for AI artwork. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Project code offers MIT or Apache-2.0. Fonts, icons and other assets retain their notices. Claude is a separately governed hosted model. Local software and compute; Anthropic API usage has separate charges. [Source](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Current documentation says the machine files have not yet been sewn on real hardware. Treat stitch export as a testing-stage workflow. The README and embroidery guide differ on the availability of credit-based AI access; the own-key route is the documented common path.

### How it uses AI

An embroidery design application combining vector editing and digitization with Claude-assisted artwork creation and image redrawing. [Source](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An embroidery design application combining vector editing and digitization with Claude-assisted artwork creation and image redrawing. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Developing a motif, choosing thread colors and planning stitch sequences for machine embroidery. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows the interface, example designs and a browser demo. These are author examples; no sewn production sample was verified in this review. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the official macOS universal DMG, Windows x64 MSI/portable package or Linux AppImage/deb/rpm. For source development, use the documented Rust toolchain. Configure your own Anthropic key for AI artwork. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the official macOS universal DMG, Windows x64 MSI/portable package or Linux AppImage/deb/rpm. For source development, use the documented Rust toolchain. Configure your own Anthropic key for AI artwork.

```sh
cargo run --release -p vectorcraft
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

1. Choose a hoop and draw or request a motif with the Claude design command.
2. Edit paths, assign fabric/thread choices and inspect stitch order and density.
3. Export a supported machine format such as DST or PES and test on scrap material before committing a finished textile.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Rust 1.97+ for source builds; packaged app for normal use; Anthropic account/key for AI generation.
- **Platforms:** Published packages cover macOS, Windows and Linux; FreeBSD is a source route. Browser demonstration has feature restrictions, including font download.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Project code offers MIT or Apache-2.0. Fonts, icons and other assets retain their notices. Claude is a separately governed hosted model.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Project code offers MIT or Apache-2.0. Fonts, icons and other assets retain their notices. Claude is a separately governed hosted model.
- **Cost:** Local software and compute; Anthropic API usage has separate charges.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Current documentation says the machine files have not yet been sewn on real hardware. Treat stitch export as a testing-stage workflow. The README and embroidery guide differ on the availability of credit-based AI access; the own-key route is the documented common path. [Source 1](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/Stitchwright/StitchFoundry/blob/main/README.md)

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Building explorable lessons and audiovisual explanations using DOM, SVG, canvas or Three.js scenes.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-21. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/LICENSE)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Speech alignment can be approximate. Optional assistant credentials and remote calls are separate from static lesson playback. No independent installation or classroom-use review was completed.

### How it uses AI

A framework for interactive narrated lessons: TypeScript scenes are synchronized with script beats, neural speech synthesis and optional conversational assistance. [Source](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

### Introduction

A framework for interactive narrated lessons: TypeScript scenes are synchronized with script beats, neural speech synthesis and optional conversational assistance. [Source 1](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

### What it is good for

Building explorable lessons and audiovisual explanations using DOM, SVG, canvas or Three.js scenes. [Source 1](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

### Demo & examples

The official optimizers lesson and Hugging Face demonstration show the intended narrated interactive format. [Source 1](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

### Install

Install Node.js 22+, Git and pnpm, install the workspace and build it. Preview a supplied lesson silently first; use the offline route with Supertonic 3 and FFmpeg when adding narration. [Source 1](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Node.js 22+, Git and pnpm, install the workspace and build it. Preview a supplied lesson silently first; use the offline route with Supertonic 3 and FFmpeg when adding narration.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm build
```


```sh
pnpm lesson preview --silent --lesson lessons/optimizers
```


```sh
pnpm lesson new my-lesson --lesson lessons/my-lesson
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

1. Preview the example lesson and inspect how script beats address scene actions.
2. Create a lesson, edit its scene and narration script, and run the lesson checks.
3. Choose an offline or hosted TTS provider, generate speech and test timing and interaction before publishing.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

- **Hardware:** The offline Supertonic package is documented at about 123 MB. Total RAM, VRAM and full project disk minimums are not documented.
- **Software:** Node.js 22+, pnpm and Git; FFmpeg for offline audio; Supertonic 3 or a separately configured supported TTS service.
- **Platforms:** Browser playback and a Node-based authoring workflow are documented. A complete native Windows/macOS/Linux support matrix is not documented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 project code; Supertonic, Qwen and any other selected model have their own terms. Hosted ElevenLabs/Hugging Face services require separate access.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 project code; Supertonic, Qwen and any other selected model have their own terms. Hosted ElevenLabs/Hugging Face services require separate access.
- **Cost:** Local compute for the offline path; optional hosted TTS or assistant calls may incur charges.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/scienceetonnante/tangible/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/spaces/dlouapre/tangible-optimizers)

## ARDY

Motion capture & character animation · AI choreography and dance composition · Avatars, digital humans & lip sync · Games & production pipelines

First complete guide in this library, including established software newly reviewed here; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Exploring directed character movement and motion-control interfaces for animation or game prototypes.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-21. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official project page and repository provide motion videos and an interactive demo route. Published clips are documented evidence, not our own test. [Source](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Create a compatible Python environment, install a matching CUDA-enabled PyTorch build, then install the demo extra. Obtain the selected ARDY checkpoint and approved access to the gated Llama text model before launch. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 project code includes separately credited MIT LLM2Vec code. ARDY checkpoints use NVIDIA model terms; Meta Llama-3-8B-Instruct is gated under its own terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. A demonstrated 4090 setup is not a minimum-GPU specification. Access to the text model and the selected body/checkpoint assets must be resolved before installation.

### How it uses AI

An autoregressive diffusion system for interactive human-motion generation conditioned on text, paths, keyframes and sparse joint constraints. [Source](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

### Introduction

An autoregressive diffusion system for interactive human-motion generation conditioned on text, paths, keyframes and sparse joint constraints. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

### What it is good for

Exploring directed character movement and motion-control interfaces for animation or game prototypes. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

### Demo & examples

The official project page and repository provide motion videos and an interactive demo route. Published clips are documented evidence, not our own test. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

### Install

Create a compatible Python environment, install a matching CUDA-enabled PyTorch build, then install the demo extra. Obtain the selected ARDY checkpoint and approved access to the gated Llama text model before launch. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create a compatible Python environment, install a matching CUDA-enabled PyTorch build, then install the demo extra. Obtain the selected ARDY checkpoint and approved access to the gated Llama text model before launch.

```sh
pip install -e ".[demo]"
```


```sh
python scripts/run_demo.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

1. Launch the local demo and select the checkpoint.
2. Provide a motion instruction, waypoints or joint constraints and inspect the generated sequence.
3. Iterate on constraints and use the documented motion output route for downstream work.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

- **Hardware:** RTX 4090 is the documented tested GPU; a minimum VRAM, system RAM or total disk requirement is not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+; PyTorch 2.4+; CMake 3.15+ and a C++17 compiler. The documented test setup uses Python 3.11. Optional TensorRT has additional driver and package requirements.
- **Platforms:** The documented tested configuration is Ubuntu 22.04 with an RTX 4090. Windows and macOS support for the full pipeline is not established by these sources.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 project code includes separately credited MIT LLM2Vec code. ARDY checkpoints use NVIDIA model terms; Meta Llama-3-8B-Instruct is gated under its own terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 project code includes separately credited MIT LLM2Vec code. ARDY checkpoints use NVIDIA model terms; Meta Llama-3-8B-Instruct is gated under its own terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)

### Limitations

A demonstrated 4090 setup is not a minimum-GPU specification. Access to the text model and the selected body/checkpoint assets must be resolved before installation. [Source 1](https://github.com/nv-tlabs/ardy/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/ardy/)


A visual assistant that combines text with pointing, scribbles and masks to drive segmentation, inpainting, image generation and optional DragGAN-style edits. [Source](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

### Introduction

A visual assistant that combines text with pointing, scribbles and masks to drive segmentation, inpainting, image generation and optional DragGAN-style edits. [Source 1](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

### What it is good for

Exploring interactive object selection and directed image editing in a research-oriented Gradio workspace. [Source 1](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

### Demo & examples

The README embeds mask editing, DragGAN and audio-conditioned image demos. It also records suspension of the online demo, so live access is not assumed. [Source 1](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

### Install

Follow the official INSTALL guide in a compatible Linux environment, obtain the required model_zoo assets and install the selected tools. Start with the basic tool set rather than loading every optional model. [Source 1](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Follow the official INSTALL guide in a compatible Linux environment, obtain the required model_zoo assets and install the selected tools. Start with the basic tool set rather than loading every optional model.

```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python -u app.py --load "HuskyVQA_cuda:0,SegmentAnything_cuda:0,ImageOCRRecognition_cuda:0" --port 3456 -e
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

1. Launch the local interface and upload a test image.
2. Select a region or draw guidance and invoke the configured editing tool.
3. Inspect the result and use only the additional tools whose dependencies and licenses have been resolved.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

- **Hardware:** At least 17 GB GPU memory is documented for the basic HuskyVQA/SAM/OCR tool set. RAM and total disk minima are not documented.
- **Software:** Linux; Python 3.8+; PyTorch 1.12+; CUDA 11.6+; GCC/G++ 5.4+. Husky uses a delta checkpoint requiring the corresponding original Llama assets.
- **Platforms:** Linux is the documented installation platform; other native platforms are not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 integration code. Llama/Husky, StyleGAN, ImageBind and diffusion assets have separate licenses and access conditions.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 integration code. Llama/Husky, StyleGAN, ImageBind and diffusion assets have separate licenses and access conditions.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/InternGPT/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

Current source still says the project is under construction. ### Introduction

A storyboard workspace that sends still and image-to-video generation to ComfyUI while organizing scenes, takes, characters and shot approval. [Source 1](https://github.com/storybored-app/storybored/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

```sh
docker compose up -d
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/storybored-app/storybored/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open localhost:8600, connect the engine and select a model pack suited to its GPU.
2. Create scenes/shots, write descriptions, compare takes and approve the chosen images.
3. Generate optional shot clips and export the board as an animatic MP4.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/storybored-app/storybored/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Author tiers start lightweight still generation at 6–11 GB VRAM, video at 12–15 GB and larger engines at 24 GB+. Character training calls for a 24 GB-class card. No complete RAM/storage minimum is supplied.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+/Node 20+ for source builds or Docker; external ComfyUI with selected models. An optional OpenAI-compatible LLM helps with script breakdown.
- **Platforms:** macOS, Linux and Windows setup instructions are supplied. ComfyUI may run on another machine; its actual accelerator compatibility governs generation.

2. Use the documented Linux Docker environment or Windows Conda route, then install the package and appropriate attention kernels. Prepare the exact chosen model weights before launching its server script.

```sh
pip install -v .
```


```sh
bash scripts/qwen_image_21/server/start_server.sh
```


```sh
python scripts/qwen_image_21/server/post_t2i.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/docs/EN/source/getting_started/quickstart.md)

1. Follow the chosen model guide and set paths/GPU in its server script.
2. Start one server on port 8000 and submit a small request.
3. Inspect the saved output, then adjust prompt, dimensions and memory settings.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/docs/EN/source/getting_started/quickstart.md)

- **Hardware:** The setup guide states NVIDIA CUDA GPU with at least 8 GB VRAM, recommends 16 GB system RAM and requires at least 50 GB free disk. Large-model routes can require substantially more.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+; CUDA-compatible PyTorch and model-specific attention/quantization operators. The guide supplies separate Linux Docker and Windows Conda instructions.
- **Platforms:** Ubuntu 18.04+ and Windows 10/11 are documented. The linked Mac application is a separate project and does not establish full framework parity.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/docs/EN/source/getting_started/quickstart.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 inference code. Qwen, Wan, MiniMax and other model families each have their own terms; some supported checkpoints are non-commercial or region-restricted.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 inference code. Qwen, Wan, MiniMax and other model families each have their own terms; some supported checkpoints are non-commercial or region-restricted.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/docs/EN/source/getting_started/quickstart.md)

### Limitations

The generic hardware floor does not mean every supported model fits. Server scripts and kernels are model/hardware specific; speed claims were not reproduced. [Source 1](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ModelTC/LightX2V/blob/main/docs/EN/source/getting_started/quickstart.md)

An agent-oriented pixel-art CLI where an AI coding agent can author text sprite grids/runs under shared palette, canvas and export rules. [Source](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An agent-oriented pixel-art CLI where an AI coding agent can author text sprite grids/runs under shared palette, canvas and export rules. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Creating consistent low-frame-count sprites and reproducible Godot 4 or Phaser asset exports from reviewable text definitions. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README provides a small pixel-grid example and preview/build commands; it is a developer workflow rather than a graphical painting app. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the tagged tool with uv or pipx, then initialize an art project inside the game repository. Configure export paths before building assets. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the tagged tool with uv or pipx, then initialize an art project inside the game repository. Configure export paths before building assets.

```sh
uv tool install git+https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool@v0.5.0
```


```sh
sprite init art
```


```sh
sprite guide
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

1. Set the project palette and canvas classes, then have a compatible coding agent draft a small sprite definition.
2. Run sprite check and inspect sprite preview output; correct the text art manually or with the agent.
3. Build and inspect the exported sheets and engine metadata in the target game.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+ (uv can provision it), pinned Pillow and an independently configured AI coding agent for the AI-assisted path.
- **Platforms:** Python CLI installation is documented; no complete tested operating-system matrix is stated. Export targets are Godot 4 and Phaser.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT tool code. The separate coding agent/model, imported artwork and game-engine assets retain their own terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT tool code. The separate coding agent/model, imported artwork and game-engine assets retain their own terms.
- **Cost:** Local tool compute plus any chosen coding-agent/model service costs.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The renderer itself is deterministic, not a neural image model; the AI role is authoring sprite definitions through the documented agent interface. There is no GUI, tilemap export or general Aseprite round trip. [Source 1](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/ginstrom/sprite-tool/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Export formats can lose editor-specific information. The default generation budget is zero and must be deliberately configured. AI generation and animation quality were not independently tested.

### How it uses AI

A sprite-processing toolkit with a local video studio that sends prompts or concept art to generation providers and converts the result into game asset sheets. [Source](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A sprite-processing toolkit with a local video studio that sends prompts or concept art to generation providers and converts the result into game asset sheets. [Source 1](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Creating short character animations and exporting timed sprites with pivots, collision polygons and engine metadata. [Source 1](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The official browser app demonstrates sheet linting, editing and export; the local studio adds the provider-backed generation route. [Source 1](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the npm package with Node 20+, make FFmpeg/FFprobe available for video, then start the companion studio and configure the chosen provider and spending limits through its private setup page. [Source 1](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the npm package with Node 20+, make FFmpeg/FFprobe available for video, then start the companion studio and configure the chosen provider and spending limits through its private setup page.

```sh
npm install -g @trebeljahr/sprite-tools
```


```sh
sprite-tools studio
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

1. Choose a prompt or concept image and configure the supported frame controls.
2. Generate or import a short video, remove its background and inspect the extracted frames.
3. Correct timing, pivots and collision shapes, then export for the chosen game engine.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node.js 20+; FFmpeg/FFprobe for Video Studio; Google or xAI provider access for generation. Existing video processing does not require generation calls.
- **Platforms:** Browser, Node CLI and MCP surfaces are documented. A complete tested native OS matrix is not stated.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT toolkit code; Google/xAI services and generated/imported assets retain separate terms. Local sprite algorithms are distinct from hosted model inference.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT toolkit code; Google/xAI services and generated/imported assets retain separate terms. Local sprite algorithms are distinct from hosted model inference.
- **Cost:** Local processing plus separately charged provider generation; imported-video processing avoids those generation charges.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/trebeljahr/sprite-tools/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

Neural inverse rendering reconstructs reflective-object geometry and BRDF material properties from multiple photographs. [Source](https://github.com/liuyuan-pal/NeRO/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

Neural inverse rendering reconstructs reflective-object geometry and BRDF material properties from multiple photographs. [Source 1](https://github.com/liuyuan-pal/NeRO/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Recovering meshes and material maps for object relighting and digital-asset experiments. [Source 1](https://github.com/liuyuan-pal/NeRO/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The official project page and repository show reflective-object reconstruction and relighting examples. [Source 1](https://github.com/liuyuan-pal/NeRO/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the repository requirements, nvdiffrast and raytracing, obtain the documented datasets or prepare a custom capture, then run shape and material stages in order. [Source 1](https://github.com/liuyuan-pal/NeRO/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the repository requirements, nvdiffrast and raytracing, obtain the documented datasets or prepare a custom capture, then run shape and material stages in order.

```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python run_training.py --cfg configs/shape/syn/bell.yaml
```


```sh
python extract_mesh.py --cfg configs/shape/syn/bell.yaml
```


```sh
python run_training.py --cfg configs/material/syn/bell.yaml
```


```sh
python extract_materials.py --cfg configs/material/syn/bell.yaml
```

### First project

1. Prepare verified copy and screenshots in content.js.
2. Ask the agent to plan scenes, then inspect keyframes and edit the scene data.
3. Render and encode the approved timeline; inspect captions, screenshot content and sound.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/bigeye0x0/bigeye-sticker-video/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Rendering creates several GB of temporary PNG frames. Minimum system RAM and GPU/VRAM needs are not documented.
- **Software:** Node 22.4+, Chrome, FFmpeg/ffprobe and lsof; Python 3 with NumPy for optional soundtrack generation.
- **Platforms:** macOS and Linux are documented; other agent clients and Windows execution have not been individually verified by the author.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/bigeye0x0/bigeye-sticker-video/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/bigeye0x0/bigeye-sticker-video/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT code and original sticker assets. External AI-agent models, fonts, product images, trademarks and added music have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT code and original sticker assets. External AI-agent models, fonts, product images, trademarks and added music have separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local rendering plus optional external AI-agent costs; no video-generation API is required by the render pipeline.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/bigeye0x0/bigeye-sticker-video/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The engine is deterministic; the model contributes authoring, not video diffusion. Default layouts target 16:9 1080p30; vertical/square output requires scene changes. Silent exports fail the soundtrack-oriented loudness check. [Source 1](https://github.com/bigeye0x0/bigeye-sticker-video/blob/main/README.md)


### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/houyu998/ai-character-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Generate a body draft and inspect the front/side render and .blend file.
2. Use annotated points/lines to specify bounded shape changes.
3. Save a new version for every change and treat later rig, hair or clothing recipes as separate manual work.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/houyu998/ai-character-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Blender 4.4 tested, MPFB 2, bundled Rigify and an external coding-agent environment. Optional FaceBuilder is commercial; motion assets and hair tools are separate.
- **Platforms:** The initial body route is documented as tested on Windows. Other versions/platforms remain untested in the README.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/houyu998/ai-character-studio/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/houyu998/ai-character-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT project scripts; MPFB/Blender, optional FaceBuilder, hair tools, motion assets and the coding model retain separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT project scripts; MPFB/Blender, optional FaceBuilder, hair tools, motion assets and the coding model retain separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/houyu998/ai-character-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Scope is the runnable body draft. Stages C–H are recipes without their one-off builder scripts, and controllable expressions are unfinished; the complete showcase is not a reproducible one-command pipeline. [Source 1](https://github.com/houyu998/ai-character-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/houyu998/ai-character-studio)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/houyu998/ai-character-studio/blob/main/README.md)

Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT application code; linked geometry libraries have LGPL/MPL obligations and configured LLM services have separate terms/costs. Python 3.11+ and Node 22+ are documented. The README explicitly says uv sync is currently broken and directs users to its development installer; full workflow and manufacturability review remain pending.

### Open Garden Planner — AI agent integration · GPL-3.0

Developing AI-assisted landscape plans and spatial visualizations with inspectable geometry.
An embedded MCP interface lets an external AI agent inspect, visualize, export and optionally edit metric garden plans using token-gated geometry tools.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. GPL-3.0 app code; the LLM/agent is an external dependency, not a built-in generative model. The Windows 10/11 installer and Python 3.11+ source route are documented. The full agent setup, output accuracy and hardware needs still need review; ecological and structural outcomes are not guaranteed by the drawings.

### Roomler AI Avatar · Apache-2.0

Exploring narrated product demonstrations that keep source screen footage distinct from generated presenters.
Local voice and talking-face models create presenters beside a real screen recording, with an LLM planning the edit and captions.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Apache-2.0 orchestration; VibeVoice, InfiniteTalk/Wan, portrait models and Claude have separate terms. The README describes a 24 GB NVIDIA GPU, 32 GB RAM and roughly 75 GB disk, with Windows 11/WSL2 or Linux. Full setup, model-term and output-reliability checks remain pending; author use is not independent evidence.

### House Walk Kit · MIT

Prototyping a walkable architectural scene and comparing room-layout alternatives.
An AI coding agent converts a floor plan or written brief into an editable Three.js house with browser navigation and plan views.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT template/plugin code; asset packs and external coding agents have separate terms. Node 18.17+ and Git are documented, with about 500 MB free storage. Uploaded conversational material is visible to the selected assistant service; full setup, scale accuracy and licensing review remain pending. A walkthrough is not a construction plan.

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **OpenDesign** · needs-license-review: The root file identifies Apache-2.0 but changes operative wording in sections 6 and 9. A GitHub SPDX match does not resolve whether the standard grant is intended; the prepared guide remains local pending clarification. [Source](https://github.com/nexu-io/open-design/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **FastGS** · excluded: The root MIT file coexists with LICENSE_ORIGINAL.md, which restricts covered Gaussian Splatting source to non-commercial research/evaluation. The README retains inherited-license obligations. The existing library assessment is refreshed; no new eligible guide is added. [Source](https://github.com/fastgs/FastGS/blob/main/LICENSE_ORIGINAL.md) [Source](https://github.com/fastgs/FastGS/blob/main/README.md)
- **Montage AI** · excluded: The complete current software license is PolyForm Noncommercial, so it does not qualify under this library’s open-source-software-only scope. [Source](https://github.com/mfahsold/montage-ai/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **PaperCraft-AI** · needs-license-review: The repository describes an interesting craft workflow, but the attempted software-license endpoint returned no license. Source availability alone does not grant reuse rights. [Source](https://github.com/OshadhaPalliyaguru/PaperCraft-AI)
- **Neural Surface Priors** · needs-license-review: The root MIT license is present, but the integrated 3D Gaussian Splatting/PermutoSDF workflow needs a resolved inherited-source license review. Attempts to retrieve the expected component license paths returned 404; no blanket clearance is claimed. [Source](https://github.com/WJakubowska/NeuralSurfacePriors)
- **MotionNet** · needs-license-review: The GitLab project overview describes AI choreography, but the attempted LICENSE path returned 404. Its topic/overview license label is not a substitute for reviewing the complete grant. [Source](https://gitlab.com/Roxanne_Ardary/motionnet)
- **Stylized Neural Painting** · needs-license-review: The complete README identifies CC BY-NC-SA while the root license endpoint is identified as CC0. The conflicting scope was not resolved, so no eligible guide or lead is published. [Source](https://github.com/jiupinjia/stylized-neural-painting)
- **Heliosonic Sonifier** · excluded: The README expressly calls this a documentation-only repository and says the application source and executables are proprietary. A GPL badge does not establish released open-source software. [Source](https://github.com/HugoPaquete/Heliosonic-Sonifier)
- **XRMoGen** · excluded: The full combined license contains an Apache grant for the wrapper but non-commercial Bailando and Jukebox software terms for the dance-generation workflow. No unrestricted complete route was verified. [Source](https://github.com/openxrlab/xrmogen/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **StereoCrafter** · excluded: The software license permits inference only for academic, research and educational purposes and prohibits production/commercial uses. This is not an open-source software grant. [Source](https://github.com/TencentARC/StereoCrafter)
- **Neural Holography** · excluded: The software grant is limited to non-commercial academic research; public code is insufficient for open-source eligibility. [Source](https://github.com/computational-imaging/neural-holography)
- **ProcMatRL** · excluded: The Adobe research license restricts use to non-commercial research, so the software is outside the open-source-only scope. [Source](https://github.com/adobe-research/ProcMatRL)
- **HunyuanVideo-Foley** · excluded: The custom community license defines a territory excluding the European Union, United Kingdom and South Korea. The software grant is not unrestricted open source. [Source](https://github.com/Tencent-Hunyuan/HunyuanVideo-Foley)
- **Moonlight OMR** · excluded: The Apache-2.0 repository is archived, and its current README says it has no release, is not ready for end users and has fragile legacy dependencies. Archived repositories are not eligible additions under the library intake rules. [Source](https://github.com/tensorflow/moonlight)
